tbmm-2020-07-16sit05spe0399par0397	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of the law, I tried to discuss this matter a little bit today when I was talking about another law, so I'd like to say a few words about the relationship between the state and the law. In Turkey, it is generally thought that the state sometimes goes outside the law. I think it's a completely wrong proposition. It's not the state that's gone against the law, it's something else now. For what it's worth, dear friends, in both politics and sociology, there's always this distinction: the state is different from the mafia. What makes the state different from the Mafia is that it's in law and legitimacy. You know, the structure you call a state is a monopoly on violence in the most general sense. So you make a contract with society, the constitution, you cleanse society of violence, and then you only use those violent devices within the limits of law and legitimacy. If you're using violence as a state outside the law, you're something else now. Thus, the state has gone outside the law. It is a meaningless, irrational proposition from beginning to end. Look, dear friends, don't get too theoretical, I won't let you get caught up in philosophy this time of night. Let me give you an example -- I don't want to give more examples of my own personal life, but -- I'm afraid I'm one of the lawmakers whose immunity has been removed. In 2016, I had just arrived, I only had two cases; I wish I had more. Because some of our deputies had 60 or 70 of them, and I was only here for three months, and in three months, you immediately removed a few arguments, immunity. Until 2018, I was tried, and they said, "I've had two insulting cases." In 2019, my party nominated me for the mayor of Bingol, when I was a Diyarbakır MP. Of course, when I was elected for the third time, in 2018, our lawyer applied. Our deputy was re-elected, he gained immunity. The court board has halted my trial, which is my constitutional right, which is not the 14th article of the Constitution I'm accused of, not of terrorism; it is an insult, it's a clause in the sentence. He stopped my trial, and then my mayoral nomination was announced, and I went to Bingol. Two weeks passed, and my lawyer panicked, and the warrant was issued for your arrest, and they retributed your judgment. What's that like? My candidacy for mayor has been cleared, and they've already started the trial. If we win the city, they'll appoint trustees, they'll prepare for it. Anyway, I went and saw the judge and the lady; there's a young lady judge. The first thing I said was, I even said it shouldn't be that ugly, 'cause they've been at our door with the police or something like that, like we've committed major crimes. I'll go, see a judge, see the lady, see what the situation is. We went, I saw the judge, and I asked the judge, "Hakime Hanim -- my first promise -- do you realize that what you did was a crime, that it was a violation of the Constitution? As an MP, I have immunity now, and you're trying to get a warrant to prosecute me on the MP with immunity; the Constitution is clear. Do you know what he told me? He said: "But Mr. Deputy, the prosecutor has objected, and we have stopped your trial before." I smiled, and I could only tell the judge that you are the head of this court committee, not the prosecutor! You sent a militant prosecutor there; even the judges are terrorised, afraid of themselves, indeed. In the end, I was tried, 11 years, 11 months and 20 days, and if I had 12 months, I would have lost my MP. We're dealing with such an outrage. Now, my dear friends, look, as I said, it's not about the state going outside the law. If the government breaks the law of its own routine... Yeah, there's a cop's baton, we see; there's a soldier's boot; we see people calling him my judge, but there's no such thing as a government. You're always making us enemies of the state, in fact, by corrupting all the institutions of the state, by taking the state away from the law, you're evacuating this state. And thank you all for listening so late.
